ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e2341	Mr. Minister! My question to It reads: 259/M How do you assess the fact that the recent decisions taken by the independent Bundesasylsenate to grant Kosovo refugees asylum in Austria remove negative first instance decisions?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e2368	Please, Minister of State. – I can say that the Federal Minister came 3 minutes too late because a meeting of the main committee was held, which apparently only ended at 9 a.m.. - Please, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e2383	Mr President. Thank you for the excuse and for justifying my pleasure. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to answer your question as follows: The first instance of the asylum authority is the Federal Asyl Office already established in 1991 in accordance with the Asylum Act 1997. The Bundesasylamt is an administrative authority with responsibility for all asylum applications filed in Austria. The independent Bundesasylsenat decides on appeals against decisions of the Bundesasylamt. It is no longer part of the Ministry of the Interior, but is part of the Federal Chancellery. I am convinced that the first instance of the asylum authority, namely the Bundesasylamt, has decided correctly in relation to the respective decision-making period. Nor does it contradict the fact that the second instance comes to a different decision at a later stage due to the change in reality, due to the change in political and actual conditions. And that is exactly what I see in this debate and discussion. The decision of the first instance has been taken under other political omens than the decision of the second instance has now been taken. I therefore assume that the decision of the independent Bundesasylsenate is one that is primarily guided and guided by the current dramatic events in Kosovo and around Kosovo. On this decision of the independent federal asylsenate and some judges in this independent federal asylsenate I am not taking a decision and I do not think I am entitled to such a judgment either.

All the other countries have not yet agreed to such a reception action. We will, however, endeavour to achieve at our further meetings and discussions that all 15 EU Member States take a similar attitude to Austria and Germany.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e2835	Please have a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e2850	Deputies! I would like to point out that last year we discussed together the new citizenship law in Parliament. Among other things, a new § 10a was introduced, which regulates that one has to prove German knowledge if one is to receive Austrian citizenship. After a long debate, we decided that we should not have to prove this German knowledge in the form of an examination, but by confirming the attendance of appropriate courses or by allowing local officials to check the knowledge on the basis of the applicant's contact with the respective authority. Until today, that is, until mid-April this year, that is about three and a half months, I am aware of 20 cases in which the award of citizenship has not been made due to lack of German knowledge. Of these 20 cases, 17 were in the state of Upper Austria, two in the state of Vienna and one in the state of Burgenland. There has been no such decision in the other Länder. There are currently no statistical records in Tyrol.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e4261	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e4277	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I would like to take a very detailed look at this, and I would also like to explain to you the current legal situation. In principle, I believe it should be noted that, in each asylum procedure, the individual case submitted to the Authority must be examined in the light of the applicable legislation, in the light of the individual situation of the applicant. In connection with the question you have asked, there is a long-term jurisdiction of the Administrative Court, which sets out the conditions under which the refusal to pay the military service or the desertion of asyl-legislative relevance may be established. For example, the Administrative Court has stated that the appointment to the military service does not in itself constitute an aylene-relevant persecution. The flight to the military service for the purpose of convoying it could be relevant only if the convocation was made on account of one of the reasons mentioned in the Geneva Convention on Refugees or, for such reasons, a threatening arbitrary punishment for refusal of military service would be more severe than against other nationals. – This is a knowledge of the administrative court of 21st century. April 1993, which I quoted. There is also a knowledge of this complex of the Administrative Court of Justice of the 29th century. ==References== At that time, as an occasion, the complaint of a national of the Yugoslav Federation was based on Albanian nationality, who had submitted in his application for asylum that he had to expect a multi-year imprisonment or even death penalty for not complying with the order for convocation. In this respect, the Administrative Court stated that the relevant question is whether the authorities of the home Member State are pursuing a different practice in relation to the convocation of compulsory Albanian nationality compared to the convocation of members of other ethnic groups, in particular Serbian, both in relation to the convocation of military service to themselves and in relation to the circumstances under which it is to be conducted, and whether the sanctions imposed in the home Member State against objectors and desertors differ in relation to the members of Albanian nationality compared with those of other ethnic groups. These parameters relate not only to any disparity between the different groups, but also to any differentiation of the consignees for the reasons mentioned in the Geneva Convention on Refugees. For this reason it is clear to me that the Bundesasylsenat and the Bundesasylamt are at first instance obliged to draw the relevant conclusions on the basis of these decisions.

In terms of content, the increased monitoring and monitoring of the Member States in relation to human rights, justice, parliamentary democracy and freedom of the media. That is budgetary autonomy for the Parliamentary Assembly, enforcement of the monitoring of judgments of the European Court of Human Rights by the Parliamentary Assembly, establishment of a codecision procedure by the Parliamentary Assembly in conventions, agreements and protocols, strengthening the role of the Secretary-General in order to act immediately in conflict situations, wider application of the system of qualified majority in the Ministerial Committee, abolition of the veto on most issues and transparency. ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e5067	The next speaker is Mr Dr. Schwimmer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e5088	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The Council of Europe was born 50 years ago as a child of a vision. Today, in view of the terrible events in Kosovo, it is difficult on the one hand to recall this vision uncritically, on the other hand, this vision is not only valid, but is still valid precisely because of the terrible events. This vision was and is one of Europe's peaceful values, united by the common values of democracy, the rule of law and human rights. In my view, the tragedy of Kosovo and the whole of Yugoslavia is proof of the need for full implementation of this vision, which was created by the even greater tragedy of the Second World War. This vision, implemented in 1949 by Winston Churchill, Alcide Degasperi, Robert Schuman, Paul Henri Spaak and other major Europeans, is not only a historic great act, but a still valid political commitment. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The Council of Europe has already achieved much in the 50 years of its existence. His greatest achievement is undoubtedly the European Convention on Human Rights, which Andreas Unterberger recently called in the "Press" "with the possibility of successfully calling against his own state bodies, probably the most sensational progress in human history since the invention of book printing". In addition, the Council of Europe has created more than 170 other conventions, making several thousand bilateral agreements unnecessary. The Council of Europe is also attacking hot iron, such as the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Human Rights in the Application of Biology and Medicine, including an Additional Protocol on the Prohibition of Human Cloning, or the Framework Convention on the Protection of National Minorities not ratified by Austria for too long ago. Austria has ratified a total of 87 of these conventions and has signed 19 more. Among the achievements of the Council of Europe are not only legal and legal instruments, but also other practical international-related actions such as the action against intolerance, racism, xenophobia and anti-Semitism, cultural initiatives such as highly respected Council of Europe exhibitions, European youth centres in Strasbourg and Budapest, or, unfortunately, in Austria, the European Foreign Language Centre in Graz, a European Council institution. In 1949 the Council of Europe began with ten Member States. In 1989, before the fall of the Iron Curtain and the Berlin Wall, it was 23. Over the last ten years, the Council of Europe has managed to spread its principles throughout the continent with a huge expansion. With Georgia it becomes 50. in a few days already being 41 member countries. At the same time, the few white spots on the map of the Council of Europe are characterized by unfortunately remaining conflict zones, unresolved problems or even the most difficult democratic and human rights deficits, as they occur especially in Yugoslavia and Belarus. Despite its achievements and its important tasks, including the deepening of its principles in all the Member States, the Council of Europe suffers from the fact that, in addition to other European institutions such as the European Union or some transatlantic institutions such as the OSCE, it has come under the background. Europe as a political term is used today as a synonym for the European Union of the 15 and not the Council of Europe of the 40 or 41. But we need this larger, this whole Europe without new walls and without new graves. We must believe in the strength of the values of democracy, the rule of law and human rights and also convince those who are still away. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] That is why the Council of Europe must become what the first summit of the Heads of State and Government, which took place at the initiative of Alois Mock in Vienna, has said: The Council of Europe must become the outstanding political institution of Europe, which has taken up the new democracy freed from communist oppression as an equal partner. The dialogue within the Council of Europe as a pan-European political forum must become a central instrument of policy, stability and peace on our continent. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP, with representatives of the SPÖ, the Greens and the Liberal Forum.]] In order to become more visible, the Council of Europe must also seek more practical results, which can be perceived by the citizens of the Member States. The Council of Europe's policy must be more at the service of the Member States and their citizens, as has long been defined in the action plan of the second summit of Heads of State or Government, as well as in many reports by the Parliamentary Assembly. It is, by the way, a special advantage of the Council of Europe that it has such a strong parliamentary dimension and that the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe can in fact act as a representative of the population of the 41 Member States in the near future. There are truly significant proposals here, such as a new social security strategy, programmes for the protection and protection of children, joint action against terrorism, corruption or organised crime and prevention of drug abuse. Finally, to improve efficiency and to optimise the potential of the Council of Europe, to intensify cooperation with the European Union and the OSCE, and to make optimum use of all possible synergies. The European Union must also be involved in the work of the Council of Europe as a pan-European political forum. On the other hand, accession of the European Union to the European Convention on Human Rights would also be a success for the European Union against its bodies. We can put it, desirable. For me, the Council of Europe and the European Union are children of the same vision, two concepts that do not contradict, but complement and remain valid. I also have a birthday wish of the Council of Europe to Austria. Peter Schieder spoke of the remaining conventions as a whole, and I would like to make one quite specific point: The Convention, which has not yet ratified Austria, is also part of the European Charter of Regional and Minority Languages. It would be nice if this House, the National Council, could approve the ratification of this Convention still in the year of the anniversary. [[It is in favour of the ÖVP, the Greens and representatives of the SPÖ.]] In the 43 years of membership, Austria has contributed much to the work of the Council of Europe, with Karl Czernetz a President of the Parliamentary Assembly, with Lujo Tončić and Franz Karasek two Secretary-Generals. Large parliamentarians such as Hermann Withalm, Bruno Pittermann, Wolfgang Blenk, Marga Hubinek and Ludwig Steiner and others have represented Austria in the Council of Europe. ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e5408	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! If you read through the history of the Council of Europe and above all the speeches about the hour of birth 50 years ago, which the founding fathers of the Council of Europe held, you can see in it the vision that is still valid today: the vision of a common Europe without war under the title "Never again war!"a Europe of diversity, but at the same time one of Europe's tolerances, a Europe that protects minorities that protect rights, a Europe that is trying to learn from its cruel history precisely this century. Austria has been a member of the Council of Europe since 1956, and – President Fischer and some of my previous speakers have already mentioned it – Austria and, above all, Austrians – unfortunately, few women so far – have been taking a very active part in the development of the Council of Europe in recent years. This is probably also the case, because especially for small countries of the Council of Europe, the Council of Europe still has great relevance, even if they are not members of the European Union. In my view, for several reasons, the Council of Europe has a very special role to play: On the one hand, it shows in practice that Europe is more than Western Europe, that Europe is more than the 15 Member States of the European Union, which are very often regarded as Europe in the population and which have political relevance and political power in Europe. The importance of increasing political relevance to the Council of Europe is seen in the fact that even the 50. The anniversary of the Council of Europe is very low in the media and very little interest and participation is shown. I think this is a pity for a number of reasons. In addition to the fact that the Council of Europe is soon made up of 41 states and is thus truly a whole body of European culture and the community of states, it also has something special, namely, in addition to the Committee of Ministers, the representation by parliamentarians, which meet four times a year as plenary, but much more often as committees, i.e. an active participation of freely elected representatives, but also a participation of non-governmental organisations. Now on a point which has not yet been raised here, but which seems to me to be particularly important: Civil society is not only important to the Council of Europe, but is actually integrated into practice. As early as 1952, non-governmental organisations were given the opportunity to take part in the Council of Europe, the possibility of a consultative status was granted, and this civil society in the form of non-governmental organisations could participate relatively easily and also participate very fully in the committees of the various European Council committees. Of course, the Council of Europe is well-known – and this has been mentioned here several times – for its work in the field of human rights, for its work against racism, for its work on the protection and rights of minorities, but also for its work in the field of environmental protection, for its work on the diversity of regions and also for its work in the field of social cooperation. For all these areas, there are committees in which there is a great deal of dedicated work done by many Members, but which is not very much appreciated in the public and often in their own parliaments. This is probably the reason why, very often, when it comes to the practice of using its labour force as an individual Member, when it comes to directly falling to fertile soil, it is decided to do so – and since each of the colleagues who are represented in the Council of Europe must get their noses on its work in the national Parliament. Unfortunately, the Council of Europe usually has a very limited commitment by the individual Members. Now I am referring to the reform of the Council of Europe. There is also a great deal of good news from colleagues, which I can only stress from my point of view. In this context, however, we must also say: Paper is patient. In part, excellent conventions have been created. However, they have often not only been ratified, but their compliance is often not specifically monitored. I'm thinking about monitoring. There is a real need for teeth to be shown that this instrument is intended to be more direct. It is only by better control, by better monitoring, that countries which are in concrete breach of the directives and the objectives of the Council of Europe can be brought directly and more quickly to bear on their responsibilities. In my view, this has not been done enough so far, and it must therefore be taken very carefully that, as a result of the enlargement of the Council of Europe, which is soon to include 41 Member States, the Council of Europe will not be a honey ration association, an association in which the principles of the Council of Europe themselves are not respected by those countries which are already members, and one looks only at the fact that one is in the process, but does not really control whether all the directives, all the principles, all the values of the Council of Europe are respected accordingly. A short while ago, on the practice of the committees, as I have seen it and as I believe it is good practice of the Council of Europe. In contrast to the national committees, there is indeed more to the word and attitude of the individual, the Member who sits there. It is a serious debate on Members' requests, including those of very small groups such as the Greens. There is a climate of tolerance, there is a climate of acceptance of different opinions, often even if one does not come to common decisions. This is a quality of discussion, so that, as President Fischer said in his introduction, the Council of Europe should be something like the school of democracy. I can show a concrete example of how to act against racism and tolerance, for example, in other specialist committees. The Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, in which I am represented, had planned to hold a conference in Montpellier. It was actually all relatively far organized. In France, regional elections were held, and in the province of Montpellier, representatives of the Front National, i.e. representatives of Le Pens, came to the government, to the head of the regional administration there. In the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, at the request of some of the Socialists who have also been supported by me, we have been concerned about whether the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection should hold a conference in a city with a well-known right-wing politician at its head. We have decided not to do so – including taking into account discussions that took place in this region. We then cancelled this conference and tried to communicate the reasons for this. I think it is important that the other committees also take into account the fact that it is not just about the subject that is being discussed directly, as in this case on environmental protection, but also about respecting all the other values of the Council of Europe. ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e5794	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! At the same time, the result is very encouraging from the point of view of the Austrian budget, because it will significantly improve our net payment position. It has also succeeded in making the Community's financing more fair between Member States. It is a very essential aspect, and that was also a central point of the discussions, because it is permissible to deal with the question "What is fair?" There are differences of views, depending on the Member States from which they come. Finally, the Agenda 2000 decisions have made it possible to create the necessary financial room for enlargement in the next decade. [[President Dr Neisser shall take over the chairmanship.]] The timely agreement on Agenda 2000, ladies and gentlemen, is a great success of the German EU Presidency, for which our German friends deserve the appropriate recognition. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The fact that Austria was able to achieve a very encouraging result in the Agenda 2000 in the financial field in particular has, not least, to do with the fact that, from the very beginning, the course was set politically right in Austria itself. Austria has clearly stressed its net interests from the outset, without neglecting the necessary pan-European solidarity. In Austria, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to stress this in particular, we agreed very early on with the partners within the framework of the financial compensation, i.e. with the countries and communities, namely already on 21 January. In March 1997, an agreement was reached between the financial compensatory partners. This agreement sets out the Austrian position on the future EU financial regulation. That is why, from that point of view, I considered this to be so important, because, as Minister of Finance, I am of course dealing with the whole of Austria, but because of the agreements that countries and communities also participate in the contribution payments, I also thought it necessary to reach a clear negotiating mandate with a goal and at the same time to have the consent of the countries and communities and not just those of the Federal Government. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] I consider this attitude to be extremely important, especially from the point of view of the federal state. ==References====External links== We set two main points in March 1997 and it was not so clear from the point of view of two years ago that such positions would ultimately prove to be attainable in the context of the discussion process. First of all, I believe that the upper limit on own resources should not exceed 1.27 percent of the EU's gross social product – this definition has been seriously challenged by some southern European countries – and Secondly, when we accept the principle that Austria will remain net payers, we aim to reduce the net position. In no way can the agreement with countries and communities occur that our net pay position worsens. We have achieved these goals, ladies and gentlemen, and I also told the financial compensatory partners yesterday! [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] This shows that, as a relatively small member country in the European Union, working together in the country itself can be successful if territorial authorities and the responsible political parties join forces for a goal. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In Austria, we have not only decided at an early stage on a promising position for the preservation of our interests, but also worked hard for this success. We have, for example, thoroughly analysed our financial position – but not only ours, but also those of our EU partner states, because I have from the outset expressed the view that it is only possible to claim fairness, whether or not in the interests of one's own country, that it is only if one's demands are structured in such a way that others are not overwhelmed. This too is an attitude that is necessary in the European Union. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! During our EU Presidency, we played an important role in the development of the concept that has finally become more and more persuasive in the European Union. This concept of real expenditure stabilisation was developed during the Austrian Presidency and under Austrian leadership. I would add, in a modest way, that it was the experts of the Federal Ministry of Finance who, in these activities, in this creativity, also the development of a third way between the hard net pay position of the capping and the hard net pay position, that it should not become less, that it was possible to design this scheme. That is why I would like to thank all of my house's employees on these questions for their enormous and so successful work. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Under the heading of real expenditure stabilisation, it has succeeded in implementing the principle that EU finances have to develop in line with national budgets and therefore must support fiscal consolidation in the Member States. The money of the European and thus also the Austrian taxpayers will be treated sparingly. This situation contributes significantly to significantly improving the Austrian net pay position, as measured by gross domestic product, between 1999 and the last year of the financial perspective, namely 2006. The net contribution from Austria to "EU 15" will improve from 0.43 percent of gross social product in 1999 to 0.31 percent of gross social product in 2006. The significant savings for Austria will be effective from 2002 onwards, as the changes in the own resources system will then enter into force and become increasingly effective in subsequent years. The economical handling of taxpayers is also reflected in the fact that annual payments from the EU budget, as measured by the EU 15 GNP, will fall from 1.13 percent to 0.97 percent in 2006. The figures from the EU budget for the "EU 15" in 2006 are at the same level as in 2000. – I understand this by real expenditure stabilisation. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In particular – I don't want to stop you for long because a lot has already been reported about it – the following structure of the main headings results: The expenditure for heading 1, common agricultural policy, however, has been sufficiently cost-effective. Agricultural expenditure, in turn at 1999 prices, in the last year of the forthcoming financial perspective, i.e. in 2006, will be almost at the level of 2000. At the same time, the EU agricultural reform has been continued – admittedly a little slower than planned, but more socially acceptable and therefore also feasible. – Hiezu will give a detailed opinion to Federal Minister Molterer. Although the funding areas – and this is the case indeed – are being streamlined in the EU, Austria will receive no less funding from the Structural Funds than it has been in the new period from 2000 to 2006. This proves, as I mean, the wise and quite skillful negotiation. Austria will receive around 700 million schillings per year for the Community initiatives in the next Structural Funds period – that is 5 billion schillings over this period – from EU funds. It has been recognized that, especially with regard to the history of the European continent, with regard to the border of prosperity, which runs east of Austria, the geographical length of the Austrian border to the reform states is almost as long as that of Germany. The fact that a small country has very special problems in the development of its own regions has been accepted by all in the final phase of the negotiations. Another success for Austria is the change in the INTERREG programme, which, in part, has suffered from the need to find partners beyond the border and to establish co-financing, which was not always easy because of the economic situation in the reform states. Secondly, In the other areas of expenditure, which go beyond agriculture and the Structural Funds, particularly internal and external policies and administrative charges, the budget has also been sparingly budgeted. At the same time, however, – and this is very important – has been taken into account accordingly the needs of future-related tasks such as research and education. In this context, I would like to point out that, under the Austrian Presidency of the Council of the EU, agreement on the Fifth Framework Programme for Research and Development has already been reached on this subject. In particular, the Minister of Science deserves a positive mention. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In addition to preparing the "EU 15" and its policies for the next few years, promoting the pre-accession candidate countries was a second very central issue in the context of the Agenda 2000. The new financial preview therefore also provides for funds for these countries so that the conditions can be created in the candidate countries to support and speed up the acquisition and implementation of the acquis communautaire. 22 billion euros are intended for this, because, ladies and gentlemen, it makes sense for the Eastern European states to be slowly, but purposefully, guided by Western European standards, it makes sense that the border of prosperity between East and West is not established forever, and it makes sense to give the people of Eastern Europe a perspective for the future in their countries, because this also reduces the pressure of migration. That is why I think all these efforts by the Union are so essential. [[Congratulation with SPÖ and ÖVP as well as the Abg. Dr Gredler.]] High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! If, for the period from 2000 to 2006, the savings for Austria – as measured by the net payment position of the year 1999, i.e. 0.43 percent of GNP – are measured, the savings for the whole period is 13.6 billion schilling. The annual savings will grow to 4 billion shillings a year by 2006, the year in which this will fully enter into force, for 2006, i.e. for 2006 and, of course, in further succession. Compared to the Commission's proposal – and this is even more crucial – that is, to the initial entry-level discussion, Austria's savings in this period are even 17 billion schilling. The reason for the improved net payment position lies not only in the changes I have already mentioned to the Fund, where, I believe, we have come across understanding of the needs of our Republic, but also and above all, with other factors. The stabilisation of EU expenditure leads to a decoupling of budget development from the development of EU GNP. The conversion of Austria's contributions to the European Union from the VAT base to the GNP component relieves Austria about 700 million schilling a year. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! In particular, Austria has been able to reduce its share of the co-financing of the British discount to a quarter, which will enter into force in 2002. This brings a discharge of 1.3 billion schilling annually. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Even within the net payer, ladies and gentlemen, Austria needs no comparison. In none of the net payer countries, the reduction in contribution benefits is a quarter, as is clearly reflected in the net position of Austria. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! As important as figures and financial successes are for the public budget, I believe that we should not lose sight of the idea of Europe. Europe has taken impressive steps towards integration and cooperation over the past four decades. Europe is actually a philosophy, and from a continent from which up to the middle of this century there were two world wars, at least in a sub-area has become an internal, a strong, a stable economic area that gives its people prosperity and peace. We are lucky to be living at a time when these changes in Europe are moving forward rapidly. We have experienced the end of the division in blocks, we are currently experiencing the creation of a single currency, and we are preparing to include my dear ladies and gentlemen, new countries prepared for accession. But the dramatic events in Kosovo show that much remains to be done. We cannot and must not tolerate human rights violations in Europe, and dictatorships have no place on our continent. So I am the Minister for Finance of the Republic Austria welcomes the financial outcome of Agenda 2000, and I think it is even more important that the European Union has also demonstrated by the Berlin Agenda Decisions that it is operational and operational and that it is determined to lead our continent a good way in the future. ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e6023	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The main cornerstone and central part of Agenda 2000 was and is the reform of the common agricultural policy. In some cases, the fact that the reform of agricultural policy has been synonymous with Agenda 2000 has even occurred. You may appreciate the importance of reforming this important sector of the economy. The reform of the common agricultural policy has been discussed emotionally and politically in Europe and Austria. This is no wonder, it is the basis of future development for the next seven years of an entire economic sector, even, ladies and gentlemen, even more, namely a sector which is responsible for our livelihoods, for our food and for our habitat. The basic objective of the common agricultural policy of the European Union is the European model of agriculture, the European model of agriculture adopted unanimously by the Ministers of Agriculture in November 1997 and adopted unanimously by the Heads of State or Government. We want multifunctional agriculture, i.e. a agriculture capable of fulfilling all the functions that society requires of it. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] This means not only the obligation to produce high-quality food and food, but also, of course, the fulfilment of all other functions: the social, the regional, the ecological function to mention only a few. Secondly, in Europe, and hence also in Austria, we want a comprehensive management of our country, i.e. a management and thus also the task of caring for, for example, the landscape in all regions, including in the less-favoured areas. For Austria, the mountain regions are of particular importance. Ladies and gentlemen! Thirdly, we want sustainable agriculture, i.e. agriculture, which is capable of fulfilling its functions in the long term, and thus agriculture that can operate in respect of the laws of nature. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! We also want a competitive agriculture that is able to exist on the market, which is able to cope with fair conditions on the market. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] In the area of milk market regulation, we succeeded in extending the milk quota at least until 2006. The reform of the milk market regulation does not begin in 2000, but only in 2005. From this point on, there is also a new income compensation for our dairy farmers. From 2005 on, the increase in quotas will be linearly 1.5 percent in all EU countries. As early as 2000, specific quotas are beginning to grow in some Member States of the European Union. Austria has been able to convert 150 000 tonnes of unexploited milk quota into a delivery right and thus offer prospects for farmers in the dairy sector, for example by the success of making dairy cows extensible in the mountain regions, which means that this has contributed to the stabilisation of incomes. Aumayr: Congratulations!]] There will also be an intervention system in the future. The premium rights have been preserved for Austria. The income compensation premiums are increased, and there are specifically Austrian regulations such as those which are of vital importance in the mountain regions of western Austria. In the field of crops, intervention prices are not, as planned, reduced by 20 %, but by 15 percent. The set-aside as a mixed control instrument is preserved. There will be a uniform crop premium, only for durum wheat, protein plants and starch potatoes, there will be specific supplement schemes. I also say very openly that a regulation on renewable raw materials has not yet been reached in the field of arable crops. Of course, this remains an agricultural policy objective, as I see a key issue for the future strategy in the development of renewable raw materials and energy sources. However, we fear that the reductions in intervention prices and export support will logically also reduce market prices. [[Abg. Dr. Drinkl: The leaser doesn't know that anymore!]] Moreover, in Austria we have much higher animal welfare and environmental standards. We are in favour, but then we cannot compete with the world market prices, because we have to fail to comply with these standards elsewhere. In addition, we have much smaller agricultural structures in Austria than they have our co-competitors. According to the surveys, the Austrian population wants this small and medium-sized agricultural structure to be maintained in the future. [[Abg. Aumayr: But you ruin them with your policy!]] This is certainly a competitive disadvantage on the European internal market. According to a Wifo study, some 660 000 people or 20 percent of all employees in Austria are employed in agriculture and in the downstream and downstream sectors. Therefore, after the Agenda 2000 negotiations, Austrian replies to Agenda 2000 are also necessary. [[Abg. Aumayr: National measures!]] Mrs Aumayr! [[Cheerfulness and applause with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] [[New call of the abg. Aumayr.]] In particular, the second pillar of the European agricultural policy, rural development, offers us a number of new opportunities, such as: Environmental support can be strengthened and safeguarded in the long term. With ÖPUL 2000, we have a programme which offers farmers much better environmental support, including from the financial point of view, and therefore needs to be increased financially. That is why I also ask the Minister for Finance to make part of the saved contributions to the European Union available to Austrian farmers at national level. We have the opportunity to introduce a base support for miners in the integrated rural programme. Austria will probably be the only one of the 15 EU Member States to introduce this base amount. We therefore want to support small-structured rural agriculture in Austria. Only by this is it possible for our Alpine valleys to remain functional. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] We now also have the opportunity to grant investment support – Minister Molterer has already mentioned this – also for the ancillary farmers. In this respect, there is no longer any difference between full-time and secondary-employees. Both forms of farming can benefit from investment promotion. For the first time, it is contained in an EU programme that also the forestry sector, such as the conversion of inferior forest areas, can be supported by the European Union. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] There is another area to address here: It has enormous importance for Austria for the renewable raw materials. Austria is the country of Central Europe which has the highest forest content. There are a lot of reforestation residues, and there is also a lot of wood growth that is not currently being used. This means not only a possible increase in income for farmers and forestry farmers, but also a very large number of jobs in this area could be created in Austria. If the total increase in wood in Austria – a study by the Ministry of Social Affairs – could be used, there would be about 40 000 additional jobs, especially in the field of renewable energy. Austria has opposed the degression of size, but unfortunately most of the European social democratic governments have rejected the degression of size. As already mentioned, specifically Austrian measures are the amount of the base for the miners and the calcin premium – which will only be offered in Austria – but also the extensification premium for dairy cows in the mountain area. And the conversion of 150 000 tonnes of unused D-Quote into A-Quote is also an advantage for the Austrian farmers because they delivered the Austrian quota by nearly 100 000 tonnes in the last marketing year and therefore had to pay "super levy". With this Austrian response to Agenda 2000, the Austrian People's Party wants to provide the Austrian farmers and people in the rural regions with the necessary support to meet the new economic challenges.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e13437	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! In the international economy, it is absolutely customary to request conditions for these procurement contracts in the context of certifications. This also makes sense because, for different order levels, for different differentiations of the contracts, different special qualifications of the companies are needed in order to get orders. There is therefore an international standardisation procedure of ISO in Geneva, the International Organization for Standardization, which is also supported by the Austrian Standardisation Institute, and on the basis of accreditations, certification bodies are now awarded ISO 9000 and other standards, for example. So far, so good. I believe that this process is correct because the requirements of the contracts to be awarded cannot be met by all companies. Example: Those who want to deliver to the European automotive industry today as suppliers can only do so if they have ISO 9000 or a similar certification. In my opinion, the quality marks given by the Chamber of Commerce are a question of competition. Dr. Mock's been talking about market economy. He is right, that is the most successful system, but Dr. Mock also knows that Austria is relatively backward in the question of competition and that it is necessary to join the European Union in order to break down structures that are well-crusted – we are not yet at the end of the process. I am not going as far as Haigermoser or Schweitzer have done here to speak of protective funds, but I am wondering whether the sections or trade associations which award these quality marks are the right level. Because there's one thing that's certain: If there is already a compulsory membership in this country – and I have repeatedly said that I do not think it is right – but if it already exists, that means that the trade union has all its members to represent. So let us leave any certifications, let us make any quality marks of accredited certification bodies that work outside the chamber and thus do not get into the smell that they would give individual companies orders. I note once again that it is clear that from a certain size, from a certain complexity of contracts, the one signing the contract requires that only those companies which have the appropriate certification can participate. That's right, too. These certificates of the Chamber of Commerce, which, in their own right, assume that all members should and should be represented, which I believe they do not do in large part, are the wrong way. Ladies and gentlemen! The Liberals will therefore agree to this request for a deadline, because I think it is worth discussing it in committee, but not in the section of how they use the liberals who have filed claims for protection, because this is an accusation of crime. If someone accused me of demanding "protection money", I'd go to the judge and complain to that person for honorous insult, because that's an absolutely honorable act. A very last comment has been added, Mr Dr. Stummvoll and Mr. President Maderthaner: If one makes access to the industry via a qualification barrier "bound trade", then one actually no longer needs a seal of quality. So let's just decide which companies are successful or not, and let's get rid of the tied trade mostly!

ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e15052	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister Prammer! Mrs. Federal Minister Gehrer! Ladies and gentlemen! All surveys confirm that young women want to reconcile work and family. They wish themselves children, but they also want to be employed, earn their own money, be independent. Most young women, as shown in these surveys, want two children. In fact, however, reality remains behind this desire. Why is that so? – It is often evident in practice that a life with children and simultaneous work is still difficult. There are not enough childcare facilities in many communities, the working hours are anything but family-friendly, and the cooperation of the fathers often leaves to be desired. In this situation, many women choose to have a second child. We Socialists are trying to counter this and create a framework that facilitates a decision for children. Our job is not to give families a few thousand schillings for a few years. What we want is a child-friendly climate and the opportunity to reconcile work and family. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] We have therefore tabled a number of legislative proposals in connection with the discussions on women's desire, i.e. some time ago, which we are taking at first reading today. I would like to make a brief presentation of these requests: These are applications which, on the one hand, are intended to make it easier for all working mothers to stay in the workplace or to ensure that, if they choose a child, they do not lose their jobs. In addition, there are also applications that take account of the particular and particularly difficult situation of single mothers. The first application allows workers to reduce working hours if they have to take care of them. This application is generally formulated. He therefore refers not only to the care of grandchildren, but also to the care of disabled or sick relatives. This request is very important to us because we know that it is difficult for someone to leave the profession for a long time, for him or for them to walk later. It is also intended to ensure a continuous course of insurance that the person concerned does not lose insurance periods. What would be desirable and what we are seeking in connection with a major reform of pension rights is a better calculation of care periods, both at the level of the pension and at the time of retirement. Another request concerns those mothers who – for whatever reason – cannot or do not want to give the father of their child. However, they should be able to receive the grant of the benefit if they undertake to repay the grant. Now it is known that this grant is drawn in by the father, so that women who do not call the father have no opportunity to receive this grant. But they are very often those who need this financial contribution particularly urgently. I have heard with pleasure that there is an agreement between the Ministry of Social Affairs and the Ministry of Family Affairs that this request should be adopted. [[Abg. Schaffenrath: Surprise!]] I think this is very positive because it is intended to help a group of women who really need it. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Gaugg: This is inconceivable! Ideas theft!]] The next request concerns the conversion of the obligation to complain when taking part-time work. [[Abg. Gaugg: Nothing happened except theft of ideas! Ideas theft of the most mischievous way!]] Another request concerns the extension of the retention period after the Karenz period. [[Abg. Gaugg: For half a year we've been talking about it! Application for Oillinger! They don't even generate for it again!]] [[Further calls to the liberals.]] [[President Dr. Neisser gives the bell sign.]] This is a request that is very important to us and that we have, unfortunately, been unable to reach agreement so far. The extension of the retention period to 26 weeks will ensure that women who opt for a child will be able to return to their work if they return from the karenz, and will be able to resume their work. This is very important for us, because we are constantly experiencing that, during the existing 4 weeks of retention period, women are often asked by the employer not to come first, and that they are then automatically cancelled. But it is very difficult for a woman with a small child to get a job again. That is why we expect this request to be very much. We want women to be re-trained and they can continue to work as full-fledged, competitive, self-conscious workers. [[Call to the Freeman:Blablabla!]] [[Abg. Gaugg: I can't stand that anymore!]] It is sometimes argued that women are played out against each other. Of course, the karenz representation also wants to retain its job, but it is important for us to give the signal that women who opt for children actually have job security. The last request is to compensate for the disadvantage of single mothers who do not have the possibility of sharing leave with the father. Single mothers, but also fathers will be expected to complete the 18th. The child's life in addition to the completion of the second year of life may also receive and, where appropriate, the grant. Ladies and gentlemen! These are the five proposals that we are making at first reading today. In addition, there are a number of ideas, plans and measures that we want to achieve. I would like to acknowledge that in the negotiations there were a number of points which were important to us, including the creation of an independent claim for fathers and the possibility of flexibilising the claim for leave. These are first steps that are important to us, but they are only first steps for us. I was just recently at an event with Minister Bartenstein. He was very much in favour of the fact that women should be able to reconcile work and family. I am very happy about this, but I believe that we can make it dependent not only on the good will of entrepreneurs, but also on the need for women to have legal claims. Main: A method of independence!]] – I know there are great differences of opinion between us. In any event, we will be able to prevent such demands from being implemented. Another point on which there is a current discussion – women’s issues are a cross-sectional matter – is the reform of marriage and divorce law. The current discussion of the last few days has actually shown what the social conscience of our coalition partner looks like on this issue. I miss your social conscience. It is an intolerable state that women who have done housework for years, who have raised children, should stand before nothing after divorce. It is not only in exceptional cases of hardship that there should be maintenance rights for these women in order not to let women fall into the poverty trap. Finally, I believe that when we talk about the situation of women and families, at a time when war is in place in Kosovo, where women are experiencing terrible things, we have not been left without comment. I would therefore like to thank the two Federal Ministers for the fact that they have also launched an action "Women against war" which my group and I very much support. Thank you.

In my own field, hematology, an exact diagnosis without molecular biological examinations would be impossible. However, the release of genetically modified plants has not yet been achieved. I therefore particularly welcome the final chapter of the report, which dealt with educational, research and economic consequences. They take all aspects into account, but they do not want to see Austria as an outsider, but as a co-designer on the way to the third millennium. My group therefore welcomes this report very positively.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-04-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00166_d7e19292	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Genetic methods are also in use in prenatal diagnostics. Several hereditary diseases – you have also noted this – are diagnosed. There are, of course, problems in this respect. Intensive research is being carried out on genetic therapies of diseases such as leukemia. Recently, the first successes are reported. Genetic engineering must not be considered unethical from its specific nature alone. Technology as such is valueless. It is crucial, in fact, how we deal with how we people use them and how to use them accordingly. [[Abg. Ing. Langthaler: That's not true!]] These must, of course, be observed and examined from case to case in relation to ethical values. Just as it can be unethical to act, it can be unethical not to act. Efforts to improve the nutrition situation in developing countries through genetic methods, for example through the development of pest-resistant plants or food with improved nutritional composition, are aimed at reducing suffering. Contributions to the reduction of energy and raw materials consumption, as well as the amount of waste in numerous manufacturing processes, replacement possibilities for the disposal of pollution or the development of sustainable farming methods in agriculture include genetic engineering applications in favour of nature. To avoid using these possibilities would be unethical – and not vice versa. By the way, genetic engineering pursues the same objectives as traditional breeding methods have been used for millenniums. They are only achieved more targeted, faster and efficient. Of course, genetic technology also includes the potential of abuse. [[Abg. Ing. Langthaler: It is rare to cross potatoes with snow bells!]] This is not a question at all, ladies and gentlemen. That is why there is a need for a constant ethical accompaniment to show the boundaries. Unfavorable objectives and dangerous applications must be made impossible. However, the sensible and necessary use should find our support. I would just like to point out a request from Mrs. Dr. Petrovic – 399/A. It requires that § 70 of the Genetic Engineering Act be avoided. This section states that the physician responsible for gene analysis, if, in order to assess the occurrence of a gene analysis, it is necessary to involve relatives of the person under study, or if it is likely that there is a serious risk of a disease of relatives of the person under study, should advise those relatives to carry out a genetic analysis. If you give this away, you actually take your responsibility to the doctor, and that can't be it. I believe that this report contains the essentials. We agree with this report. Thank you.

The case that he fed transgenic potatoes to rats, which had previously been planted with a snowdrop gene – something, Mr Leiner, which is not really happening in nature, not even in the course of 3000 years – has led to the production of a lektin, which has subsequently caused corresponding damage to the rats to whom it has been fed, namely damage to the immune system, but also – as has been subsequently been noted – damage to the brain and damage to the stomach walls. Dr. Pusztai has not published his results for a long time because he has tried to draw attention to the problem internally first. I can tell you that I have followed this debate very closely. Dr. Pusztai, I think, was most unhappy about what he found out. He has repeatedly stressed that he considered genetic engineering to be a reasonable instrument. It is similar, as you know, in France and India. That's the pattern we're standing in front of. We are not currently facing a genetic engineering industry in the food sector, which is pursuing some altruistic goals. No, the opposite is the case! It is about maximising profits, it is about quite great power to the farmers, to the farmers, and it is about bringing them into strong dependence. That cannot be the right way for God's sake! [[Abg. Dr. Leiner: But that has nothing to do with genetic engineering!]] This has a lot to do with genetic engineering, because it is precisely about this area, about Monsanto and so on. In case of consent, I also asked for a sign. – I also notice here: Adopted by a majority. The next item is the vote on the request for the approval of the 1744 Supplements report. I ask for a sign in case of consent. – The vote will take place by a majority. We shall vote on the request to take note of the 1745 report of the health care committee's supplements. Here again, in the case of your consent, I ask for a sign. – The notice is made by a majority. We are now voting on the report of the Health Committee 1746 of the supplements. In case of your consent, I ask for a sign. – Here, too, attention is taken by a majority. We shall now vote on the 1747 report of the Supplements. I would ask for a sign in case of your consent. – The vote will take place by a majority. The report has been taken note of. The same procedure follows on from the request that the report of the Health Committee be taken into account in 1748 of the Supplements. In case of your consent, I ask for a sign. – The National Council adopts this proposal by a majority. Finally, we shall come to the vote on the request from the Committee on Health, Public Health and Consumer Protection to take note of his report in 1749.
